3. Maritime Safety
Within the General Directorate of Infrastructure, Transport and the Sea (DGITM),
the Directorate of Maritime Affairs is the heir of the Merchant Marine
ministry, created in 1929 at the initiative of M. Georges-Leygues with the mission of
supervising all the public policies applied specifically to this sector of activity, both
regarding the personal status of companies and employees (setting up of regulated
statutes) and the social regime applied to the various components of protection.
From 1982 (institution of a General Secretariat of the Sea) and especially from
1999, by virtue of the promulgation of the agricultural framework law no. 99-574, this
ministry was incorporated into the ministry in charge of Agriculture for what touches
on monitoring the common fisheries policy, and for the other activities of this sector,
into the ministry in charge of public works and transport, which became the ministry
in charge of sustainable development in 2007.

The sub-directorate of maritime safety participates, in relation with the directorate of
legal affairs, in the elaboration and implementation of the legal norms relating to
maritime signalling, maritime traffic, search and rescue at sea, the safety of
commercial and fishing vessels and the prevention of pollution by ships. It
participates, in relation with the directorate of legal affairs, in the elaboration and
implementation of the legal norms relating to the security of ships. It organizes,
drives, coordinates and controls the activity of the Regional Operational Centres for
Surveillance and Rescue and of the Ship Safety Centres, within the framework of the
prerogatives and responsibilities of the flag State, the coastal State and the port
State, including within the framework of the control of the transport by sea of
dangerous goods. It drives and evaluates the activity of the services charged with
maritime signalling, within the framework of the prerogatives and responsibilities of
the coastal State, in liaison with the pilotage service and the evolution of the services.
It maintains relations with the bureau of investigation into accidents at sea in the
event of an incident at sea. It follows the activity of the service with national
competence named "Fleet of Lighthouses and Buoys" [Armement des Phares et
Balises].

4. Energy and climate
History of the producing service
Initially responsible for "developing and implementing government policy in
the field of energy and raw materials", the Directorate-General for Energy and
Raw Materials (DGEMP) belonged, before 2008, to the Ministry of Industry
(Ministry of the Economy, Finance and Industry). At that time, it included the
"raw materials and subsoil" service, the "renewable energies and rational use
of energy" service; and the two directorates of "hydrocarbons" and "
gas, electricity and coal"1. In addition, it was responsible for monitoring the
activities of the Commissariat à l'énergie atomique (Atomic Energy Commission)
and its subsidiaries, for activities relating to
energy production and the supply of basic nuclear materials.
In 2008, it took the name Directorate-General for Energy and Climate. Its
mission is to develop and implement policy relating to energy, energy
raw materials, as well as the fight against global warming and
air pollution.

169 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Repertoire-d-archives-detaille-energie-
    eolienne-1941-1976-energie-maremotrice
It implements measures for the control and distribution of energy products and
raw materials. It ensures the proper execution of public service missions
in the field of energy. It coordinates, in consultation with associations,
economic and social partners, and with the support of all the ministries
concerned, the preparation and implementation of the French programme for the
prevention of and adaptation to climate change.

4.1 Climate
Climate archives at Météo-France172
Météo-France website
Météo-France holds tables of meteorological observations made in metropolitan
France, in the Overseas Territories and in its former colonies since the end of
the 17th century, the date of the first instrumented measurements.
This site gives access to the inventories of these archives, witnesses of the
past climate, held at Météo-France or on deposit at the National Archives, and
allows free consultation of the digitised documents online.
Contacts
archives.climat@meteo.fr

History of the producing service
Prefigured by the General Secretariat for Energy created under Vichy by the law of 27 September
1940, which disappeared as early as 1942, the general secretariat for energy which appeared in 1963 has authority
over the directorates, existing before it, which concern the various energy
production sectors. Its mission, continued by the general delegation for energy from 1973
onwards, is to implement all measures likely to ensure France's
energy supply, while steering the activities of the ministry's energy directorates.

In 1978, the administrative structures of the energy field were brought together under
the authority of a new directorate-general named the Directorate-General for Energy and
Raw Materials (DGEMP), which also inherited the delegation for raw materials
savings and the Delegation for New Energies, both created in 1975.

The remit of the Directorate-General for Energy and Raw Materials extends over the
control and distribution of energy products and mineral raw materials,
but also over the impact of the measures contemplated by the other ministries on energy
and raw materials policy. In liaison with the ministry responsible for
the environment, it puts in place any measure aimed at preventing or reducing the
nuisances caused by energy production and consumption.
Until 2009, the Directorate-General for Energy and Raw Materials (DGEMP) was
attached to the ministry in charge of Industry.

Changing its name to the Directorate-General for Energy and Climate (DGEC), it was
attached to the central administration of the Ministry of Ecology, Energy,
Sustainable Development and the Sea, in charge of green technologies and climate
negotiations, when the latter was created in 2009.

The DGEC is composed of a directorate for energy and a climate and energy
efficiency service. The SD2, sub-directorate for security of supply and new
energy products, falls under the directorate for energy.


---


It comprises 6 units: 2A (Office for hydrocarbon exploration and production); 2B
(Office for security of supply); 2C (Office for the petroleum industry and new
energy products); 2D (Office for petroleum logistics and distribution); 2E
(Office for gas infrastructures); SNOI (National Service of Inter-Allied
Pipelines).

The sub-directorate ensures France's security of supply in oil and
gas through:

- the supervision of the exploration and exploitation of French subsoil
deposits, on land as at sea, in order to guarantee a rational and professional
exploitation of national hydrocarbon resources;

- the establishment of long-term partnerships with producer countries and the
monitoring of international markets;

- the constitution of strategic oil stocks and gas reserves, on the surface or
in suitable geological formations;

- the definition of an appropriate policy of investment in hydrocarbon
transport infrastructures and usage tariffs, both competitive for the
consumer and attractive for the investor;
- the implementation of a sustainable energy transition, by promoting the use of
biofuels, by checking the quality of the fuels distributed, by developing
carbon capture, storage and geological utilisation technologies for CO2 and by
promoting the development of geothermal energy.

Ministry: Industry
Directorate-General: Energy and raw materials
Directorate for gas, electricity, coal
Electric energy technical service, large dams (STEEGB).

Introduction
The STEEGB is responsible for a technical mission in the fields of the production,
transport and distribution of electric energy concerning hydroelectric
developments - dams; electricity networks and thermal power plants,
conventional and nuclear, for which it monitors the generating units, operational
surveillance, site security, and protection plans for nuclear power plants.
This accession of 31 items consists of documents relating to the general organisation of
nuclear energy (site locations, long-term development plan, electric energy
exports...) 1952-1995; to files of declaration of public utility
(DUP) for nuclear and thermal power plants (1972-1994); to accompaniment
programmes for major nuclear construction projects (1975-1994); and 4 other
major projects (1975-1995) - (classification by site).

Contents
Art 1-7: General matters concerning nuclear energy (site locations, long-term
development plans for nuclear power plants, energy exports, preliminary
studies, territorial development, conference of the chairmen of local information
commissions), 1952-1995.
Art 8-25: Files of declaration of public utility for nuclear and thermal power plants
(classified by category then chronologically), 1972-1994.
Art 26-31: Accompaniment programmes for major nuclear and
electric construction projects (classified by site), 1975-1995.

4.2.1.3.2 Nuclear Safety Authority


---


In 1973, control of nuclear safety in France fell to the Central Service for the
Safety of Nuclear Installations (SCSIN), attached to the minister in charge of
industry.
This service became the Directorate for the Safety of Nuclear Installations (DSIN)
in 1991, attached to the two ministers in charge respectively of industry and of
the environment.
The ASN was then constituted, at the national level by the DSIN and, at the
regional level, by the Divisions of Nuclear Installations (DIN) placed within
the Regional Directorates for Industry, Research and the Environment (DREAL).
On 22 February 2002, the ASN saw its field of action extended to radiation
protection. The Directorate-General for Nuclear Safety and Radiation Protection
(DGSNR) then replaced the DSIN, and the Divisions of Nuclear Safety and
Radiation Protection (DSNR) replaced the DIN.
The ASN reported to the ministers in charge respectively of industry, of the
environment and of health.
On 13 June 2006, law no. 2006-686 on transparency and security in nuclear
matters transformed the status of the ASN by conferring on it that of an
independent administrative authority, henceforth independent of the ministers.
All the staff and resources of the former DGSNR and the former DSNR are now
present within the new ASN.
The change of status, in June 2006, of the ASN into an independent
administrative authority (IAA) does not fundamentally modify the scope and
content of the missions until
307 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Repertoire-d-archives-detaille-
    Administration-centrale-Centrale-nucleaire-de-1204
then exercised by the Directorate-General for Nuclear Safety and Radiation
Protection (DGSNR) and the divisions of nuclear safety and radiation
protection (DSNR).

4.2.1.4 Hydrocarbons330
Descended from the National Office of Liquid Fuels created by the law of 10 January
1925, the fuels directorate appeared in 1940 as one of the important elements in
the organisation of a ministry of Industrial Production. Responsible for the supply
of oil, petroleum products, hydrocarbons and alternative fuels, this directorate,
created as early as the French State regime, plays a strategic role both of
regulation and of management of this important sector of industrial activity.
It underwent a progressive structuring and was reinforced at the time of the
creation of the Directorate-General for Energy and Raw Materials in 1978.
The service for raw materials and the subsoil, and more particularly its mining
legislation office, is responsible for missions complementary to those of the
directorate for hydrocarbons, for the issuance of research and exploitation
permits for hydrocarbons.


---


329 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Repertoire-d-archives-detaille-
    Administration-centrale-centrale-nucleaire-de-4471
330Sources https://www.siv.archives-nationales.culture.gouv.fr/siv/NP/FRAN_NP_007431
In 2008, the Directorate-General for Energy and Climate was created within
the Ministry of Ecology, Energy, Sustainable Development and Territorial
Planning.
It inherited part of the responsibilities of the Directorate-General for Energy
and Raw Materials of the Ministry of Industry, and of the Directorate for the
prevention of pollution and risks, notably as regards global warming and
air pollution.
It develops and implements policies relating to energy, energy raw
materials, the fight against global warming and air pollution, and the
reduction and prevention of nuisances caused by energy production and
consumption.
In consultation with associations, it coordinates the economic and social
partners and participates in the French programme for the prevention of and
adaptation to climate change jointly with other ministries.

4.2.1.5 Gas, coal
The Liberation, with the nationalisation of the companies in charge of gas and
electricity, led to the creation of a directorate bringing together the already
existing electricity directorate, and the gas service of the mines directorate.
The directorate for gas, electricity and coal, created in 1970 by the merging of
the directorate for gas and electricity and the coal service from the mines
directorate, retains the same responsibilities. Its mission
is to organise the public service of gas and electricity by preparing and having
executed the texts concerning the organisation and operation of the gas and
electricity services. It also ensures the supervision of the State over the
electricity and gas industries. On the other hand, questions of gas supply and
production are not within its competence, but fall to the directorate for
hydrocarbons.

Gas

The Serre de la Fare dam project (on the Loire) was abandoned on 31 July
1991. It was contested by environmental associations along with other
hydroelectric projects of the Loire catchment basin, such as the Veurdre
dam, the Naussac 2 dam or the Chambonchard dam.

4.2.7 Brittany
Centre for information on energy and the environment (Rennes), 1973-2011
Reference number:
2449 W 1 - 103430
Content:
The accession contains elements on the operation of the structure and on
its activities: protection of the environment, awareness of air pollution,
waste management, promotion of renewable energies.
Activity reports, subsidy files, communication elements, educational
tools, presentation documents of the actions carried out (exhibitions,
conferences, thematic awareness weeks), and above all the many posters
illustrating these actions, testify that the CIELE association was an
actor in environmental education in Brittany. These actions were carried
out in schools and colleges, mainly in Ille-et-Vilaine, but also in the
other Breton departments.
CIELE was also a resource place open to the general public thanks to its
documentation centre; put its skills at the service of local authorities
by assisting them in their studies on energy control or on waste
management, in promotion operations or in participation in Monitoring
and surveillance commissions for waste treatment installations. Training
courses were offered to elected officials, teachers, local authorities
and the associative sector alike. The agreements kept - with the Regional
council, General council, City of Rennes, DIREN-DREAL - record the many
projects made possible. CIELE was in particular linked by agreement to
ADEME (Agency for the Environment and Energy Management) from 2001 to
2010.
Publication:
Departmental Archives of the Ille-et-Vilaine
20/09/2021
Information on the producer:
Centre for information on energy and the environment (Rennes)
Information on acquisition:
Custodial history:
Association under the law of 1901 arising from the national ecologist
movement "Les Amis de la terre" (Friends of the Earth), CIELE (Centre
for Information on Energy and the Environment) was created in Rennes in
1986. Its main objective was to make information on environmental
issues and energy issues available to individuals.
It had up to 7 employees and its premises were located at 96, canal
Saint-Martin in Rennes. It ceased its activities and was dissolved in
March 2011.
CIELE provided the archives of its predecessors. The whole was deposited
at the Departmental Archives of the Ille-et-Vilaine on 30 March 2011.
As CIELE exercised public service missions, part of the archives followed
the fate of the public archives and was classified in Accession 2449W.
The part relating to private archives was given the reference 274J. Four
producers appear there: the private archives of Yves Cochet and the


---

    energie-et-l-environnement-Rennes-1973-2011
archives of three associations: Les Amis de la terre of Rennes, the
Rennes association for the science shop, and CIELE.
The 2 sets contain a fine collection of posters distributed according to
the origin of the fonds. It has not always been possible to determine the
producers for the posters prior to the creation of CIELE.

---


Presentation of the content
This accession contains archives relating to the control, by the Mines
service, of mining concessions and quarries located in the departments of
Loire-Inférieure, Maine-et-Loire, Mayenne, Sarthe, Vendée. Attached to it
are certain deposits located partly straddling the departments adjoining
the current Pays de la Loire region (Morbihan and Deux-Sèvres) which
depended at certain times on the jurisdiction of the district or
sub-district of Nantes.
8 S 3 / 173-163: personnel of the Mines service
8 S 3 / 1-173: surveillance of mines and quarries

Extreme dates
1749-1976

Access conditions: Documents are consulted in accordance with the
Heritage Code, art. 213-1 to 3
Holding institution: Departmental Archives of the Loire-Atlantique

Accession 2254 W485

Regional directorate for Industry, research and the environment of
Pays-de-la-Loire

Presentation of the content
This accession concerns the monitoring of the mining sites of the
Pays de la Loire region as well as the control of the mining research
carried out. It contains the files of application for mining titles, the
documents relating to their examination, the files of abandonment of
mining sites, the environmental impact studies, the declarations of
research works, the safety instructions and the exemptions relating to
them.

Classification scheme
2254 W 1 to 34: general matters
2254 W 35 to 38: antimony
2254 W 39: gold
2254 W 40 to 43: iron
2254 W 44 to 118: uranium
2254 W 119 to 142: other ores and polymetallic sites

Extreme dates
1940-2009

Title
Prefecture of Vaucluse. Public works and transport
Extreme dates
1798-1999
Location of the fonds
Departmental Archives of the Vaucluse
Historical elements
In the 19th century, bridges and roads, together with the railways, the
main road network and the policing of rolling traffic, fell under the 3rd
office, then 3rd division of the prefecture; a special officer was
attached to the roads service. The reorganisations of 1856 and 1864
maintained a 3rd division largely devoted to bridges and roads, main
roads, as well as navigation routes, watercourses and plants. From 1871,
with the reduction of the number of prefecture directorates to two, these
attributions went to the 1st office of the 1st directorate (municipal
affairs, public works and transport). They returned to the 3rd division
in 1945, with a 1st office grouping together public works and transport,
urban planning and reconstruction, traffic service). The 1964 reform
carried out a transfer of powers to the departmental directorate of
equipment, and maintained at the prefecture, within the powers of the
SCAE (service for coordination and economic action) the coordination of
major developments and investments within the framework of the Plan.
Method of acquisition
What will be found in the fonds
Bridges and roads: personnel, auction works (1799-1940). National and
departmental roads (1798-1995). Railway (1825-1978). Aerodrome
(1937-1999). Mines and quarries (1801-1992). Drinking water supply,
sanitation (1977-1982). Electric energy (1878-1981). Hydrocarbons, gas,
pipeline (1947-1980). Collective developments and equipment (1960-1976).
Transport of passengers and goods (1906-1964). TGV
Méditerranée-South-east (1982-1999).
Accruals
Open fonds

5. Risk prevention


---


History of the producing service
The notion of Prevention of pollution and risks was mentioned in the
attributions of the minister delegated to the Prime minister in charge of
the protection of nature and the environment: "...the minister [...] has
the mission [...] to prevent, reduce or eliminate pollution and
nuisances of all kinds..." (decree no. 73-94 of 2 February 1973).
A Directorate for the prevention of pollution and nuisances (DPPN) was
created in 1975 with 6 services until 1968: service in charge of water
problems, service in charge of the problems of the sea and oceans,
industrial environment service, atmosphere problems service, solid waste
problems service, noise problems service
From 1981 to 1985 this entity became the directorate for the prevention
of pollution with 3 services and 3 sub-directorates (industrial
environment service, sub-directorate of air pollution, water service,
sub-directorate of inland waters, sub-directorate of marine waters, waste
service)
From 1986 to 1987, this directorate consisted of 3 services and 4
sub-directorates: industrial environment service, sub-directorate of air
pollution, water service, sub-directorate of coordination and
programming, industrial environment service, sub-directorate of air
pollution, sub-directorate of coordination and programming,
sub-directorate of water development, sub-directorate of the quality of
inland and marine waters, waste service
From 1988 to 1992, the directorate became the Directorate of water and
the prevention of pollution and risks (DEPPR) with a delegation for major
risks attached to the director, and 3 services - that of the industrial
environment, that of water, Clean technologies and waste service
From 1993 the DEPPR was transformed on the one hand into the water
directorate and on the other hand into the Directorate for the prevention
of pollution and risks (DPPR). The industrial environment service was
maintained and two sub-directorates were created: the sub-directorate of
products and waste and that of the prevention of major risks.
In 2008, two general directorates were created: the General directorate
for energy and climate (DGEC) and the General directorate for risk
prevention (DGPR). They inherited the attributions of the former DPPR of
the ministry of ecology and the former General directorate for energy
and raw materials (DGEMP) of the former ministry of industry.
The DGEC is composed of an energy directorate and a climate and energy
efficiency service.
The DGPR is made up of a technological risks service, a nuisance
prevention and environmental quality service and a natural and hydraulic
risks service.

In this chapter you will find some archive fonds covering the 19th-20th
centuries period. The references come from the work of Andrée Covol "Les
sources de l'histoire
de l'environnement", 2003

This accession contains the files for the creation and modification of
gas transport pipelines. They are examined by the regional directorate
for Industry, research and the environment (DRIRE) then by the regional
directorates for the Environment, planning and housing (DREAL) within the
framework of the mission of surveillance of the rules of design and
construction of the works.

Presentation of the content
This accession contains first of all the control files of the classified
installations oldest still kept by the departmental unit of
Loire-Atlantique (UD 44). It then brings together two crisis management
files relating to petroleum disasters which affected the coasts of
Loire-Atlantique: the shipwrecks of the Amoco Cadiz on 16 March 1968 and
of the Erika on 19 December 1999.

Access conditions
Freely communicable subject to the restrictions imposed by the physical

History of the producer
A few milestones in the career of Serge Antoine
- From 1954 to 1973, at the Delegation for regional planning, this ENA
graduate was the architect of the creation of the French regions and the
regional economic observatories; he was also one of those responsible
for the establishment of the regional natural parks and the coastal
conservatory
- In 1980, he was appointed secretary general of the High committee for
the environment, and became director of the review "2000" of the DATAR.
552 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Repertoire-d-archives-detaille-Serge-
    Antoine-Amenagement-du-territoire
- In 1973 the ministry of the environment was born. Serge Antoine held
various positions there
- notably head of the studies and research mission (1968-1984), then
head of the environment-development mission (1984-1991) - which allowed
him to contribute to the definition of the natural heritage, to the
birth of the environmental policy linked to regional planning.
- He created, in 1973, in the saltworks of Arc-et-Senans (Doubs) an
international centre for reflections on the future (the
Claude-Nicolas Ledoux Foundation) which organises colloquia on
technological innovations and the evolution of society. The archives of
the foundation, kept at the Departmental Archives of the Doubs, contain
the proceedings of these debates on the future.
- The creation by Serge Antoine, in 1968, of the international
crossroads of communication, in the Arche de la Défense, proceeds from
the same passion for futurology and his sense of openness. In the same
spirit, this visionary participated in the creation of the
international association and the review "Futuribles", to which he made
numerous contributions.
- Serge Antoine took part in the United Nations conferences on the
environment, from Stockholm (1973) to Rio de Janeiro (1992) and from
Kyoto (1997) to Johannesburg (2002), where the future of our world is
determined. The reports, studies, working documents, notes and
correspondence collected by this representative of France constitute a
precious source for history.
- Serge Antoine was largely at the origin of the United Nations
Environment Programme (UNEP) initiative for the Mediterranean. He became
vice-president of this action plan for the Mediterranean, known as the
"Blue Plan". The unprecedented mobilisation of multiple and so
different States around the great rescue plan for the Mediterranean is
described over time through the expert assessments, analyses, studies
but also the meeting reports, the meetings of the "contracting parties",
the protocols and the intervention programmes.
- A major challenge for the environment, demographic growth and
urbanisation are the themes of reflection and action debated at the
world summits of cities organised under the aegis of UNESCO, in Lisbon
(1994) or Istanbul (1996). Serge Antoine, who took part in these
conferences, contributed to the formation of the World federation of
united cities (FMCU).
- A scientific cooperation, through forums and publications, was
established with many research centres, such as the International
centre for research on the environment and development (CIRED), the
International centre for advanced Mediterranean agronomic studies
(CIHEAM), the Group for studies and research on the Mediterranean
(GERME)...
- Serge Antoine engaged personally in several organisations: he was an
administrator of the "Seydoux Foundation for the Mediterranean" and
president of the association "Méditerranée 2000" whose activities we can
follow through the boards of directors.
- In 1992 Serge Antoine took part in the Rio summit, as special adviser
to France.
- He was appointed representative of France to the Mediterranean
commission for sustainable development created in 1996. He chaired the
French committee for the environment and sustainable development (the
"Comité 21") and worked closely with the World commission for
sustainable development.
- He encouraged cultural cooperation initiatives through the creation of
a network of Mediterranean bookshops, the organisation of colloquia,
forums. He supported the project to protect the "100 Mediterranean
cultural sites" under the patronage of the United Nations and took an
active part in the French-speaking colloquia organised by the high
council of the Francophonie in Tunis (1991) or on the margins of the
Johannesburg world summit (2002).
- Death in March 2006.
Bernard Vuillet
Archivist at the ministry, 2008
Presentation of the content
  Serge Antoine, who died in March 2006, devoted his entire career as a
senior civil servant to the cause of the environment. His wife and
faithful collaborator, aware of the importance of the documentation and
files built up, decided to give them to the ministry of Ecology to make
them fully available to researchers and the public.
These archives, representing 42 linear metres, are of exceptional
richness: they reflect all the activity and reflection of Serge Antoine
who held positions of high responsibility in the French administration
and in international bodies.
From 1954 to 1973, at the Delegation for regional planning, this ENA
graduate was the architect of the creation of the French regions and the
regional economic observatories; he was also one of those responsible for
the establishment of the regional natural parks and the coastal
conservatory. The files kept trace the debates and the aspects of the
administrative reshaping of France.
A forerunner and man of convictions, Serge Antoine deployed tireless
energy. In 1980, he was appointed secretary general of the High committee
for the environment, and became director of the review "2000" of the
DATAR.
In 1973 the ministry of the environment was born. Serge Antoine, who
worked for its creation, held various positions there - notably head of
the studies and research mission (1968-1984), then head of the
environment-development mission (1984-1991) - which allowed him to
contribute to the definition of the natural heritage, to the birth of
the environmental policy linked to regional planning. Armed with his
relations with ministerial offices, with political leaders, he fought to
have ecological requirements accepted. He took part in reflection
groups, such as "prospective and environment" or "environment and
regional planning", multiplied his interventions in colloquia, wrote
articles in specialised reviews which are found in the archive fonds.
The files also show how, from environmental concerns, Serge Antoine's
thought extended to the protection of the cultural heritage and even
became interested in the organisation of time and leisure.
Very early on, Serge Antoine envisaged problems from an international
perspective. For regional planning, for example, his reflection
extended immediately to the European horizon. Passionate about
prospective and open to international cooperation, he created, in 1973,
in the saltworks of Arc-et-Senans (Doubs) an international centre for
reflections on the future (the Claude-Nicolas Ledoux Foundation) which
organises colloquia on technological innovations and the evolution of
society. The archives of the foundation, classified separately from the
Serge Antoine fonds, contain the proceedings of these debates on the
future in which experts from Europe and elsewhere took part.
The creation by Serge Antoine, in 1968, of the international crossroads
of communication, in the Arche de la Défense, proceeds from the same
passion for futurology and his sense of openness. In the same spirit,
this visionary participated in the creation of the international
association and the review "Futuribles", to which he made numerous
contributions.
The international dimension, Serge Antoine was going to fully implement
it by taking part in the United Nations conferences on the environment,
these world summits, from Stockholm (1973) to Rio de Janeiro (1992) and
from Kyoto (1997) to Johannesburg (2002) where the future of our world
is determined. The reports, studies, working documents, notes and
correspondence collected by this representative of France constitute a
precious source for history. Through these files gathered by a witness
and actor of the first rank, one can follow the preparations for these
forums, then their course and the orientations taken.
It was at the Stockholm conference in 1973 that Serge Antoine began to
evoke the interest of multilateral cooperation for the preservation of
the Mediterranean. He was largely at the origin of the initiative taken
by the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP) in favour of the
Mediterranean, an initiative which led to the bringing together of the
riparian States signatories of the Barcelona agreements (1975). Serge
Antoine is rightly considered the "father of the Blue Plan", this Action
plan for the Mediterranean (PAM) which is not only concerned with
fighting pollution sources, but intends to establish integrated
development and careful management of resources on the scale of the
basin. The Blue Plan, of which Serge Antoine was the organiser and the
fervent ambassador to the political authorities as well as to the
scientific community, both in France, in Europe and throughout the
Mediterranean world, occupies the largest part of this archive fonds.
The unprecedented mobilisation of multiple and so different States
around the great rescue plan for the Mediterranean is described over
time through the expert assessments, analyses, studies but also the
meeting reports, the meetings of the "contracting parties", the
protocols and the intervention programmes.
To the immensity of the task correspond the diversity and technicality
of the files that Serge Antoine patiently studied, annotated, completed.
Alongside the official texts, published reports and communications, the
historian is lucky to find, in this documentary deposit, the
preparatory notes, exchanges, drafts which shed light on the in-depth
work carried out upstream of the solemn declarations and diplomatic
meetings.
To invest himself in so many projects at once and manage this mass of
information, Serge Antoine distinguished the areas of intervention such
as the protection of the coastline, the state of marine pollution, the
management of water resources, the evolution of agriculture and the
Mediterranean forest, climate change... A major challenge for the
environment, demographic growth and urbanisation around the
Mediterranean are among the main themes of reflection and action. "The
urban fact, declares Serge Antoine, is for me at the heart of the
problematic of contemporary societies. This appeared to me many times
and, in particular, at the Vancouver conference in 1980 on habitat and
human settlements." The world summits of cities organised under the
aegis of UNESCO, in Lisbon (1994) or Istanbul (1996), show well that the
thought of the visionary applies beyond the Mediterranean basin. Serge
Antoine contributed to the formation of the World federation of United
Cities (FMCU).
As vice-president of the Blue Plan, he maintained relations with
international bodies (UNESCO, OECD...) and government representatives,
whose files echo this. Relations with the funders that are the European
investment bank and the World bank, with the European commission within
the framework of the Euro-Mediterranean programme which establishes a
partnership between the two shores of the Mediterranean. Contacts also
with the Non-governmental organisations associated with the work of the
Blue Plan, with the bodies linked to the protection and development of
the Mediterranean such as the Mediterranean water institute (IME),
Environment, development and action (ENDA), or the Mediterranean Aspen
Institute.
A scientific cooperation, through forums and publications, was
established with many research centres, such as the International centre
for research on the environment and development (CIRED), the
International centre for advanced Mediterranean agronomic studies
(CIHEAM), the Group for studies and research on the Mediterranean
(GERME)...
For Serge Antoine, the work for the protection and development of the
Mediterranean is also expressed through a personal commitment in several
organisations: he was an administrator of the "Seydoux Foundation for
the Mediterranean" and president of the association "Méditerranée 2000"
whose activities we can follow through the boards of directors.
Always at the avant-garde of ideas, he adopted the concept of
ecodevelopment formulated by Ignacy Sachs (professor at the School of
advanced studies in social sciences) around 1974, and the notion of
sustainable development officialised by the Brundtland report in 1987.
From then on, as his files show, a strategy of ecodevelopment and
sustainable development imposed itself, which he sought to implement
with his Mediterranean partners.
In 1992, the Rio summit, in which Serge Antoine took part as special
adviser to France, nourished his reflection and gave new dynamism to the
Blue Plan. Serge Antoine was appointed representative of France to the
Mediterranean commission for sustainable development created in 1996. He
chaired the French committee for the environment and sustainable
development (the "Comité 21") and worked closely with the World
commission for sustainable development.
As his interventions and writings prove, he had long had a global
conception of development. He did not consider the environment as one
domain, but associated its protection with economic and social
development and respect for cultural practices.
From his entry into the ministry, this man of dialogue and openness
acted to establish education about the environment as well as better
mutual knowledge between cultures. He encouraged cultural cooperation
initiatives (creation of a network of Mediterranean bookshops,
organisation of colloquia, forums), he supported the project to protect
the "100 Mediterranean cultural sites" under the patronage of the
United Nations and took an active part in the French-speaking colloquia
organised by the high council of the Francophonie in Tunis (1991) or on
the margins of the Johannesburg world summit (2002).
At the end of this fruitful career devoted to the protection and
enhancement of the environment, the archives left by Serge Antoine take
on an exceptional character. The files make it possible to follow the
stages of the reflection of a forerunner, the commitments and actions of
an influential personality, during nearly half a century in which
ecology gradually imposed itself as the main challenge of our survival.
Through his high functions, his relations with the scientific, political
and associative world, and above all through the breadth of his thought,
he leads us to the heart of decisions that commit our future.
The entry of the archives of Serge Antoine into the National Archives
is in line with the work of the militant and pedagogue who declared: "I
am the father of the Blue Plan, but we all own it."
Bernard Vuillet, archivist at the ministry, 2008

Producer of the archives
Louis Armand
Biography or history:
A Savoyard, "born in Cruseilles, Haute-Savoie", a village whose name he
cites sixteen times in eight pages, in the first chapter of his book
Simples propos, that is the true identity of Louis Armand, the one he
gives on the police sheets of all continents. A little forgotten by the
younger generations, we will remember of him his knowledge, his
humanity, his enthusiasm and his sincerity, fine qualities of all
epochs. Jean Ullmo, his most intimate and constant friend, said of him:
"He offered us a complete image of a man without shadow, that is to say,
devoid of pettiness. He believed perfectly in man and acted perfectly for
him. He was the model that gives confidence". A fine example of a life to
discover.
    • 1905 (17 January): Birth of Louis Armand in Cruseilles
      (Haute-Savoie), son of Jean-Marie and Marie-Clotilde Masson,
      schoolteachers.
    • 1917: Entered directly the fourth form, at the Berthollet secondary
      school in Annecy. Baccalauréat.
    • 1922: Entered the class of Special Mathematics, at the Parc
      secondary school in Lyon.
    • 1924: Passed the entrance competitions of the École polytechnique,
      and the École normale supérieure. Entered Polytechnique in October
      (would leave it second).
      [....]
    • 1929: Engineer of the Corps of Mines in Clermont-Ferrand. Personal
      scientific works on mineral waters.
      [....]
    • 1935: Prize of the Collège de France and of the National academy of
      medicine for his personal scientific works on mineral waters.
      [....]
      1945: Director of the central equipment service at the SNCF (8
      May). President of the applied research committee of the
      Commissariat for atomic energy (CEA).
      [....]
      1950: Robert Schuman launched the idea of a European Community of
      coal and steel (ECSC) (May).
      [....]
      1956: Adhesion of Louis Armand to the action committee for the
      United States of Europe of Jean Monnet (January). President of the
      nuclear energy Commission of the ECSC. Speech of Louis Armand to
      the National Assembly on atomic energy (July). Presentation by
      Louis Armand to a Commission of the Economic council.


---


553 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Fonds-Louis-Armand-1880-1989
      Nomination of the Three Wise Men (December). President of the
      board of improvement of the École polytechnique.
      [....]
      1968: Honorary president of the SNCF. First president of Euratom
      (European Community of atomic energy) (January). Euratom, USA
      agreement (June). First management report of Euratom, signed by
      Louis Armand (September).
      1973: Resignation from Euratom for health reasons (January).
      President of the Collieries of Lorraine (February). Creation of the
      Rueff-Armand committee (November).
      1973: Vice-president of the Rueff-Armand committee in charge of
      establishing a report on the obstacles to economic expansion.
      Member of the Academy of Moral and Political Sciences (December).
      Vice-president of the European Foundation of Culture. Publication
      of the report of the Rueff-Armand Commission. President of the
      Commission for action against air pollution.
      1961: Publication of Plaidoyer pour l'Avenir (Plea for the Future).
      Secretary general of the UIC (January).
      1962: President of the French Association of Standardisation
      (AFNOR).
      1963: Member of the French Academy (13 June), elected to the chair
      of professor Henri Mondor.
      1964: Resignation from the presidency of the Collieries of Lorraine.
      Agreement of the European railway networks on the installation of
      automatic coupling.
      1968: Left the board of improvement of the École polytechnique.
      Publication of the Pari Européen (European Wager) and Simples
      propos.
      1973: President of the committee for the improvement of the
      environment.
      1973: Adoption by the government of the hundred measures in favour
      of the environment, proposed by Louis Armand. Publication of Propos
      ferroviaires.
      1973 (30 August): Death of Louis Armand at Villers-sur-Mer
      (Calvados). Burial at Cruseilles.
      1968: Publication of the Leçons de choses of Louis Armand.
      1984: Death of Geneviève Armand, his wife.
Custodial history:
This private fonds was given to the Departmental Archives of the
Haute-Savoie in April 1990, by the association of the Friends of Louis
Armand constituted on 32 May 1973, after his death, with the aim of
keeping his memory and pursuing his action by spreading his thought.
Presentation of the content:
Composed of documents, photographs, films and recordings covering the
intense and varied activity of Louis Armand, a man of technology and of
the railway, great resistance fighter, academician, this fonds accessible
under the reference number 85 J, represents 6 ml and is composed of 1051
items each of which is the subject of a detailed analysis.

Bernard Dominique, ecologist militant

Reference
224 J 1-143

deposit

Date of entry

Content
Dominique Bernard, physicist, joined the Friends of the Earth with Yves
Cochet in 1977, political engagement alongside Brice Lalonde from 1981 as
departmental leader; participated in the creation of the National Greens
and at the regional level in the grouping of Political Ecology within the
Breton ecologist Federation which would become Les Verts Bretagne,
adhesion to the Greens in 1983; member of the coordination and
secretariat of Rennes-Verte; Breton delegate to the National
inter-regional council of the Greens (CNIR)
Founder and president of the Centre for information on energy and the
environment (CIELE), founding member of the Feuille d'érable and of the
House of consumption and the environment (MCE) and president of the
association Agir pour l'environnement.
Also very active in the diffusion of scientific and technical culture:
founder of a Science Shop in Rennes, member of the Group of scientists
for Information on nuclear energy (GSIEN), appointed to the French
Commission for sustainable development, anti-nuclear militant.

Access
Access conditions
free
Related sources
To be noted: Fonds of the CIELE (2449 W), Coopérative de l'érable
(184 J)
Notes
(unclassified supplement, June 2017)

Reference: FR-FEP 11AM 1-116554
Title: Dominique Bernard.
Predominant dates: 1977-2008
Actual dates: 1977-2008
Level of description: fonds
Producer: Dominique BERNARD, physicist, joined the Friends of the Earth
in 1977 and became politically engaged from 1981 during the presidential
campaign of Brice Lalonde, ecologist candidate for the presidency of the
Republic. He contributed to the regional grouping of the ecologist
movements of Brittany within the Breton Ecologist Federation and to the
creation of the national Greens which he joined at its creation in 1984.
He is a member of the coordination and secretariat of Rennes-Verte,
Breton delegate to the National Inter-regional Council of the Greens
(CNIR) but also vice-president of the Centre for information on energy
and the environment (CIELE) and member of several associations including
the Feuille d'Érable and the Group of scientists for information on
nuclear energy (GSIEN).
Extent: 0.7 m. l. (i.e. 7 boxes).
Method of entry: The fonds was deposited by Dominique Bernard at the
foundation on 30 November 2017.
Holding institution: Foundation of political ecology
Custodial history: Gathered during his years of militancy, the
collection of stickers presented in this inventory was classified
thematically by its producer. It was deposited at the FEP at the same
time as the rest of his fonds composed of documents, posters and campaign
objects.
Presentation of the content: The fonds contains documents internal or
external to the Greens whether at the national level, that of Brittany or
of the Rennes group. There are also many press reviews concerning
political ecology. Also kept are many badges, stickers, slides, magnetic
tapes and posters addressing many themes.
Classification scheme: The documents were classified chronologically to
respect the original organisation of the producer. Within each year, the
documents were grouped by theme. Many of them recur for each year: the
Greens, the Greens Brittany, the various elections. Duplicates were
eliminated as well as

554 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Fonds-Dominique-Bernard-1977-2008
many press cuttings from various newspapers when they had no link with
the ecologist movements or with ecological subjects.
Access conditions: Free in accordance with the copyright of the internal
regulations of the FEP.
Reproduction conditions: Free in accordance with the copyright of the
internal regulations of the FEP.
Language of the documents: French, German, English.
Related sources: One can also find at the Departmental Archives of the
Ille-et-Vilaine the Dominique BERNARD fonds kept under the reference
number 224 J.
Date of the description: June 2021.
Contact: Meixin Tambay
m.tambay@fondationecolo.org or archives@fondationecolo.org
Bonduelle Antoine, ecologist militant


---


Reference: FR-FEP 6AM 1-37555
Name of the fonds: Antoine Bonduelle
Predominant dates: 1989-2000
Actual dates: 1980-2005
Physical description: paper
Producer: Antoine Bonduelle began his militant struggle as early as
1968, when he joined the Friends of the Earth of Lille. The following
year, he is also found among the animators of Radio Lille 80, a free
radio broadcasting in public from the House of nature and the
environment (MNE), since become the Regional house of the environment
and solidarities (MRES). Targeted many times by police seizures, this
radio was the last to broadcast before the end of the presidency of Valéry
Giscard d'Estaing.
Antoine Bonduelle became engaged from 1984 with the Greens whose
electoral campaigns he supported, essentially in his region of
Nord-Pas-de-Calais.
In 1988, he developed with Philippe Tostain the Association for the
right to the bicycle (ADAV), which subsequently became the first
association of Lille in number of members. At the same time, he worked on
the development of the institutional and media balance of power for the
Greens, on the widening of the base of ecologist militants, on the
reflection on the logic of collective action...
From 1994 until 2003, he was associated with the cooperative research
office INESTENE (Paris) on energy and the environment, founded by Pierre
Radanne. In this framework, he carried out work for the French Agency for
energy management (AFME), the European Union, Greenpeace, the WWF or the
Green Group in the European Parliament. In this capacity he made many
modellings and formulated critiques of the French electrical system.
He founded, in 1996, the Climate Action Network - France (RAC-F), French
section of the Climate action network, of which he took the first
presidency until 2003. He continues to this day his voluntary work on the
executive board. He was, notably through the RAC, the leader of the
French environmental defence NGOs during the international conferences on
climate, notably in Berlin (COP1, 1995) and Kyoto (COP3, 1997).
He also took part in the Intergovernmental group of experts on climate
change (IPCC), as Expert Reviewer of group III (mitigation, energy
policies) of the IPCC of 2007 and 2013 as well as for the IPCC report of
2011 on renewable energies.
An engineer and journalist trained in political sciences, Antoine
Bonduelle is currently - and has been since 2005 - a consultant in energy
and environment for companies, NGOs or public authorities. He was a
member of the Economic, social and environmental council during two
terms (2010-2015, 2015-2021).
Extent: 0.8 m. l. (i.e. 8 boxes).
Method of entry: Antoine Bonduelle gave his paper archives to the
Foundation in October 2016 (accession form 11/16), to which must be
added two gifts of posters (accession forms no. 9/16 and 3/17).


---


555 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Fonds-Antoine-Bonduelle-1980-2005

Presentation of the content: The archives of Antoine Bonduelle trace his
militant career from the end of the 1970s to the beginning of the 2000s,
in particular his involvement within the Greens of the North and the
Energy commission of the Greens.
Classification scheme: The documents were classified chronologically,
according to the commitments of Antoine Bonduelle. Three books1 were set
apart in the library of the FEP.
Several eliminations were carried out:
- press cuttings and documentation on Euralille;
- generalist national press on political news;
- documentation on mobility, transport and urban planning;
- duplicates of documents of the Greens.
The fonds also contains posters which will be the subject of a later
classification.
Access conditions: These documents are private archives. Refer to the
deed of gift to see the conditions of communication.
Reproduction conditions: Reproductions within the framework of private

see the fonds of the Greens Nord-Pas-de-Calais (7AP) and the
Marie-Christine BLANDIN fonds (12AM) kept at the FEP for complements.

         Grevet Simone, association member

Accession 332 J561

Fonds Simone Grevet

History of the fonds
The Simone Grevet fonds given to the Departmental Archives in 2020 comes
from the voluntary activity of Mrs Grevet within the association for the
protection of the Croisic coastline (APLC) created in 1991 by eight
residents of Le Croisic to fight against a port project at Le Croisic.
The association aims to protect the coastline and, in particular, to
prevent constructions in deep waters in the sectors of Saint-Goustan and
Le Castouillet. It is approved as an "environmental protection
association" which allows it to take part in the action of public bodies
concerning the environment and possibly to be designated to sit in
Commissions or advisory councils.
After joining the association Défense de l'environnement et côte sauvage
(Decos) in 2004, it was dissolved in 2005.
Simone Grevet, a member of the association, gathered documents relating
to her associative activity and her centres of interest on the shipwreck
of the oil tanker Erika, its causes and consequences.
On 12 December 1999, the oil tanker Erika, cracked, broke in two off
Brittany and spilled nearly 20,000 tonnes of oil into the sea. The oil
spill affected the coastlines from the Finistère to the
Charente-Maritime, about 400 kilometres of coasts. Firefighters,
soldiers and volunteers organised themselves to depollute and try to
preserve the affected fauna and flora. The case was brought before the
courts and, for the first time in France, ecological damage was
recognised.

Presentation of the content
The documents concern the shipwreck of the oil tanker Erika in 1999 and
the ecological disaster that resulted. They consist mainly of press
cuttings and reports.
Classification scheme
332 J 1 - 9: membership of the association for the protection of the
Croisic coastline
332 J 10 - 32: documents collected in connection with the shipwreck of
the oil tanker Erika

Extreme dates
1999-2019
Linear metres: 0.73 ml
Legal status: Private archives
Communicability: Communicable

Kervern Georges-Yves, senior civil servant

Accession 20100225563

The private fonds Georges-Yves Kervern, engineer, expert in risks

Biography
Born on 9 May 1935 in Nantes. Died on 16 December 2008.
Son of Georges Kevern, maritime expert, and of Marguerite Touz. Married
to Simone Alloiteau, art gallery director (3 children)
Former student of the École polytechnique (class of 1955; graduated
ranked 12th, "Mines de France d'Outre-Mer"). Former student of the École
des mines de Paris (Entered in 1968 ranked 10 out of 13 corpsards,
graduated ranked 5 out of 13). Graduate of the Institute of political
studies of Paris 9, where he would be a Lecturer from 1968 to 1973. Corps
of mines.
He first worked at the Ministry of industry from 1961 to 1968, in the
ores and metals service, then in the minister's office.
From 1968 to 1986, he worked in the Péchiney group, as director at the
general directorate, then as president of Tréfimétaux (1980), then
president of Aluminium Péchiney (1980) and of Cégédur.
From 1986 to 1989, he was an adviser at the general directorate of
Paribas.
From 1989 to 1995, he was at the Union des assurances de Paris (UAP), as
adviser to the president then as deputy general director.
He then directed the commercial tele-insurance project Tactic. He founded
Assurland. Very versed in risk management, he directed the Cyndinopolis
project from 1999.
At the same time, he continued to teach, notably at the Sorbonne and at
the University of Lyon 3. In 1996-1997, he was general director of the
Sophia-Antipolis Foundation, then in 1997-1998, President of the
association of digital cities. From 1997, he was a member of the National
committee for the evaluation of research (economic, social, cultural,
scientific and technical fields).
To learn more website Annales des Mines 564


---


563 Detailed inventory https://ressources.histoire-environnement.org/Repertoire-d-archives-detaille-Don-
    Georges-Yves-Kervern-concernant-la
    See the archive fonds of the Institute for the mastery of risks
https://ressources.histoire-
    environnement.org/Repertoire-d-archives-detaille-Institut-pour-la-maitrise-des-risques-historique
564 http://www.annales.org/archives/x/kervern.html
Presentation of the fonds
The present fonds is a gift from the wife of Georges-Yves Kervern,
collected at his home shortly after his death and tracing an essential
part of a career with many facets.
G.Y. Kervern (1935-2008), polytechnician and mining engineer, carried out
a large part of his career in the private sector, in particular at
Péchiney and at the UAP, after a stint at the ministry of Industry from
1961 to 1968. Having become a specialist in risk management, teacher and
initiator of many projects, he had been appointed in 1997 to the National
council for the evaluation of research (CNER).
He is, with a few other thinkers, at the origin of an approach still
little known: cindynics. The private fonds G.Y. Kervern is not that of a
senior civil servant of the Environment or of Equipment; one will not
find there the missions and reports which constitute the fruit of the
successive posts, nor is it the imprint of his personal and professional
career but the "working tools" which he gathered around an intellectual
object which touches the very essence of the aforementioned
administrations.
Cindynics, which claims to be the science of danger and takes up the
Greek root of the word, was founded in December 1987 following an
international colloquium bringing together at the United Nations
educational, scientific and cultural organization (UNESCO) more than a
thousand experts in major technological risks at the initiative of the
French association of senior executives for social and economic progress
(ACADI) chaired by G. Y. Kervern. At that time, large companies were
questioning the development of a new specific function, the "Risk
manager"565, more considered and also more "modern" than the traditional
safety officer.
Accidents and industrial disasters had already been grouped and analysed
from 1980 by Patrick Lagadec, research engineer at the Econometrics
laboratory of the École Polytechnique, under the label of "major
technological risks". France equipped itself between 1984 and 1986 with
a Secretariat of State in charge of the Prevention of major technological
and natural risks held by the volcanologist Haroun Tazieff.
Cindynic reflection would progress during seminars held in 1989 and 1992
then deploy through various institutions: the European institute of
cindynics created in January 1990 whose presidents were Pierre Tanguy then
Claude Frantzen, Cindynopolis in 1999. The teaching centres also
multiplied: the École des mines de Paris which inaugurated a chair of
Industrial safety in November 2006, from the 1990s the courses of G. Y.
Kervern at the Sorbonne and in Lyon in matters of global management of
risks and crises as well as many other curricula.
G. Y. Kervern distinguishes three generations of cindynic research: the
first (1987-1994) corresponds to the question of major technological
risk illustrated by several disasters, the second (1994-2001) addressed
psychological risk by transferring the first cindynic concepts towards
civil society represented here by the family, the city, public health,
finally the third would be that of the "major anthropological threat",
that is terrorism, conflicts but also the total transformation of the
natural environment, its artificialisation by non-reversible
technologies.
The ambition of its progenitors who can present it outside its original
framework as "a new paradigm in social sciences" and an ever more
advanced formalisation to master complexity led to the progressive
transfer of cindynics from the


---


565The "Risk manager" was invented in 1956 by Russel Gallagher of the
Philco insurance company in the Harvard business review; in France the
"risk managers" are notably represented by the Association for the
management of risks and insurance in the company (AMRAE)
activities of industrial risk-managers towards the human sciences,
sociology but also the milieu of psycho-therapists who very quickly took
up its concepts.
G.Y. Kervern thus placed himself at the edge of a multitude of paths
which demand to be explored.
Contents
Files of Georges-Yves Kervern, mining engineer, expert in risks
Art 1-2: data specific to Georges-Yves Kervern, 1991-2008
Art 3-9: formalised evolution of the cindynic approach (risk
assessment), epistemology and modelling of complexity, cindynic
anthropology, 1989-2006.
Art 10-24: diffusion of cindynics: institutes and tools, teaching and
colloquia, 1987-2008.
Art 25-34: policy and management of risks: systems, organisation and
responses, territorial management, 1987-2008
Art 35-50: casual application of cindynics: risks of networks and human
organisations, natural risks, 1988-2008.

Fonds "Pierre Laurent"
Breton and Celtic research centre (CRBC), University of Western Brittany,
Brest.
2.4 Legal status
2.5 Functions and activities
Pierre Laurent learned the Breton language at fifteen. He entered the
École polytechnique in 1922.
From 1947 to 1956 he was president of Ker Vreiz, the Breton cultural
centre of Paris.
Until 1973 he was vice-president of the Federalist union of ethnic
communities in Europe.
Then elected president of the Regional union of Brittany for the
environment (URBE), he entered in this capacity the Regional economic and
social committee. He notably participated in the elaboration of the
Breton cultural charter and in the establishment of the Cultural institute
of Brittany.
He was decorated with the order of the Hermine in 1991.

History of the producer
Architect D.P.L.G, Research engineer of the INRA, complementary studies
in Linguistics, Modern Letters and Archaeology.
During the 1970s and, after 3 years in an architecture agency as head of
the drawing office, project officer in one of the first research offices
in environment (Eco-Projet) then research officer in a teaching and
research centre newly created by the National school of Bridges and
Roads (the CERGRENE, now CEREVE), he specialised in the fields of urban
morphology, operational analysis of landscapes and urban vegetations
(co-inventor of the Green Cadastre®), of environmental assessment or
integrated planning methodologies in rural or urban environments, and of
the associated cartographies.
After having been technical adviser, in charge of the democratisation of
public inquiries, noise, major equipment and the urban environment, in
the office of Mrs Huguette Bouchardeau, minister of the Environment,
from 1983 to 1984, and head of the Landscape mission (directorate of the
Protection of Nature), he participated at the INRA, between 1986 and
2008, in the integration of the environment and sustainable development
into the policies, programmes and research of the Institute, in the
enhancement and diffusion of the work and the results (creator, notably,
of the Courrier de l'environnement de l'INRA (irregular weekly in French,
15,000 copies, 50,000 readers) and followed there the relations with the
political, administrative, professional and social actors intervening in
these fields.
He was simultaneously, from September 1999 to August 2000, adviser in
charge of nature and hunting in the office of Mrs Dominique Voynet,
minister of Regional planning and the Environment.
He was, from February 2008 to March 2013, Vice-president of the National
commission of public debate (CNDP, independent administrative authority).
Administrator of the French federation of societies for the Protection of
Nature (France Nature Environnement) from 1986

Method of entry
Accession
Custodial history
Created in 1995, the ministerial History committee has the mission of
keeping memory alive and devoting itself to the study and research
necessary to ensure the conservation, enrichment and enhancement of its
heritage. It develops activities in the fields concerning today the
fields of competence of the ministries on which it depends: ecological
and solidarity transition (environment, prevention of risks, transport
and the sea) and cohesion of territories (urban planning, housing,
planning).
Jean-Pierre Raffin, born on 1 April 1937 in Boulogne-Billancourt (Seine),
is a French personality of the protection of the environment. Graduate
in natural sciences, he is doctor of 3rd cycle in comparative histology
and cytology and doctor of State in natural sciences. Before his
retirement in 2002, he held various functions at the faculty of sciences
of Paris then at the university Paris VII-Denis-Diderot. He began as a
teacher researcher in 1963 as demonstrator of practical works in zoology
(invertebrates and vertebrates); he created the teaching of ecology in
1973 with François Ramade. He then became assistant professor in ecology
from 1973 then lecturer in ecology from 1985. Since 1984, he is
co-founder and co-responsible of the stream Knowledge, Management and
Planning of Natural and humanised Spaces (CGEN) then of the department,
of the DESU and the DESS "Espace et milieux" of the university
Paris VII-Denis-Diderot (from 1996 to 1997) and finally head of the DESS
"Espace et milieux" (from 2000 to 2002). Engaged in the defence of the
Vanoise national park, he became engaged at France Nature Environnement,
of which he would be president and of which he is now honorary
president. He is at the origin of the choice of the hedgehog logo. He
occupied
numerous functions in bodies related to the environment. He was a member
of the National council for the protection of nature and of its standing
committee (from 1977 to 1989), member then head of the Bear Group
(between 1983 and 1990), then member of the High Committee for the
Environment (from 1982 to 1986), president of its Commission
"Environment and balance of species and environments", member of the
Boards of directors of the Écrins national park (from 1968 to 1989 then
from 1995 to 1997) and of the Pyrenees national park (from 1989 to 1995).
Then, he is member then president (from 2002 to 2006) of the Scientific
council of the Écrins national park, President of the College of Chairs of
Scientific councils of National parks (in 2005 and 2006), member of the
IUCN. SSC Bear Specialist Group (from 1991 to 2002). Catholic, he is a
member of the Commission "Safeguarding and stewardship of creation" of
Pax Christi since 1991, become Antenna of "Environment & modes of life"
Pax Christi - Social commission of the bishops of France in 2002,
vice-president of this antenna since 2002, member of the selection
committee Eole 2005 (from 1996 to 1997), member of the office of the
Ministry of Regional Planning and the Environment (from 1997 to 1999),
member of the Environment Council of EDF (since 1998), member of the
Commission of biomolecular engineering (from 2000 to 2002), and finally
President of the Observatory of oil spills (from 2000 to 2002). He is
the author of 15 works in the field of histochemistry and experimental
neuroembryology and 73 in the field of the knowledge and management of
the natural heritage and was appointed knight then officer of the
national order of Merit (in 1983 and 1996) then knight and officer of the
order of the Legion of Honour (in 1987 and 2000).
Candidate on the list of the Greens in the European elections of 1989,
he was a European deputy from 11 December 1991 to 18 July 1994,
replacing resigning deputies. He is a member of the Commission of the
environment, public health and consumer protection. He chose not to seek
a second term.
Appraisal, sorting and eliminations
In accordance with the archiving policy of the ministry of Ecological and
Solidarity Transition, only the files identified for their heritage
value are kept.
Classification scheme
The classification plan of the archives is built by the producers of the
archives and the archivists. In order to account for the actions carried
out within the framework of the public policies carried by the
administration, it is organised by function and activity. Also, the
current classification respects the classification plan(s) adopted at
the time of the Accession to the archives of the ministry of Ecological
and Solidarity Transition.
Accruals
Fonds not closed
Presentation of the content
The History committee constitutes a fonds of sound archives, composed of
testimonies for research in human sciences on the history and memory of
the ministry. At the present time, it has collected more than 200
testimonies from members of this administration. It has been interested in
the evolution of the professions and professional cultures, in the major
upheavals which have affected it (collection "Thematic campaigns and
professions", in the career paths of the personalities of this ministry
(collection "career narratives"). The history committee implements, with
the help of associate researchers, campaigns for collecting oral
testimonies which cover varied themes. The recordings collected serve to
feed research on public policies and the history of the administration in
France, but also to build a history of the ministry, and to keep the
memory of the professions of equipment and the upheavals
that have occurred in the last fifty years in the administration. To
these campaigns is added the Career narrative collection, created in 2004,
which gives voice to great figures of the administration, witnesses and
actors of the public policies implemented by the ministry. These
interviews, conducted jointly by researchers and members of the history
committee, return to the political and administrative upheavals of the
last fifty years as seen by senior civil servants of the central and
regional administration. After the gift to the National Archives of the
archives of France nature environment (FNE) made in 2015 by Jean-Pierre
Raffin, honorary president of FNE, the archives mission of the ministry
asked the history committee to conduct an interview of Jean-Pierre
Raffin. This fonds contains, in digital format, the sound recording, the
written transcription of the interview and the contract of conditions of
communicability

Presentation of the content
This fonds concerns slides relating to the bear and oil spills.
Oil spill.
At the General assembly of the FFSPN (now nicknamed France Nature
Environnement) devoted to "the economic value of the coastline", the
associations gathered in Brest on 3 and 5 February 1968, having in memory
the oil spill of the Torrey Canyon (1977), had questioned the
representative of the Maritime prefect on the measures planned in case of
a possible oil spill. It was answered that improbable, given the quality
of the tankers in circulation, everything had however been planned to
compensate for it. The facts would demonstrate the opposite a month and a
half later.
Then secretary general of the FFSPN and teacher-researcher in ecology at
the University Paris 7-Denis Diderot (co-founder of the teaching of this
discipline, in 1973, in this university), I then asked, after the
shipwreck, the Breton university and associative colleagues what
Parisian biologists could do to help them. The answer was to examine part
of the coastline of the Côtes d'Armor to record the impacts on the flora
and the marine flora of the intertidal zone and on the contiguous
terrestrial coastal fringe. This is how I set up a team of biologists from
different disciplines. The photographs illustrate essentially a first
mission in May 1968. On the spot we said to ourselves that it would be
interesting to ensure a follow-up to observe how the affected
environments would reconstitute themselves. This was done for certain
sites until 2009. But two questions arose: how to evaluate without having
the state of the places before the oil spill? (this is how, following the
oil spill of the Amoco Cadiz, we advocated the establishment of
observatories of the coastal environment where the evolution of
biological diversity would be monitored over the long term. This
proposal was not retained by the public authorities); how to evaluate the
part due to the oil or to the cleaning products widely used on the rocky
environments of the upper parts of beaches which by runoff flowed towards
the sandy zones of these beaches or towards the muddy zones? This was all
the more true as we also ignored the nature of the detergent products
used, for lack of the authorities responsible for the cleaning having
apparently been concerned to make an inventory of them and to keep a
record of what had been used, where and when.
The improvisation which presided over the cleaning operations of spring
1968 and the use of detergents (of which the British colleagues having
experienced the pollution of the Torrey Canyon had told us that they
could have an impact on the flora and fauna worse than the oil alone)
led the Breton biologists and association members to advocate an
anticipation of future oil spills. This concerned in particular the
equipment for collecting the oil (the only effective implements had been
the slurry tankers of the farmers of the coast and shovels and buckets
for a "Chinese-style" collection less damaging for the living
environment than the heavy public works equipment) and the installation
of truly waterproofed dedicated pits allowing secure storage before
treatment of the waste. This would be written into the POLMAR plan but
not implemented, so that after the shipwreck of the Erika (12 December
1999) the same improvisation would be noted as after that of the Amoco
Cadiz and the doubling of a marine pollution by a terrestrial pollution.

Zonabend Françoise, ethnologist

295 J 1-28569
Fonds Françoise Zonabend
[1973]-2006
by Julie Laplanche, Domnine Plume
2008
Presentation of the content
The fonds comprises the notes and documents which served Françoise
Zonabend to publish a book and a documentary on the region of La Hague
facing nuclear power. The archives are essentially documentation, working
notes and the transcriptions of the interviews she conducted.
History of the producer
Françoise Zonabend is an ethnologist, director of studies at the School
of advanced studies in social sciences. She has focused her work, among
other things, on questions of kinship and risk. Her research led her to
work on the region of La Hague and the links that unite its inhabitants
to the nuclear companies. She was part of the Commission for the public
debate on the construction of the EPR power plant570.
Custodial history
The archives were deposited by Françoise Zonabend in 2008.
Method of entry
Deposit, 2008
Access conditions
Private archives
The archives are freely communicable, except items 11, 12, 13 and 14
(interview reports). These items are consultable with the authorisation
of Françoise Zonabend.
Existence and location of originals
The fonds must be linked to the audio recordings of the interviews
conducted by Françoise Zonabend. These documents are referenced with the
audiovisual fonds, in 107 AV.

28

Air-Normand

1. Zone of identification
1.1. Type of entity
Association.
1.2. Authorised form of the name
Air-Normand.
2. Zone of the description
2.1. Dates of existence
1973 to today.
2.2. History
It was during the years 1973/1974 that pollution measurement networks
were created around the Seine estuary and in Rouen.
It was the associations ALPA (Association for the implementation of the
network of study, measurement and alarm for the prevention of air
pollution of the Lower Seine at the level of the estuary) and REMAPPA
(Association for the implementation of the network of study, measurement
and alarm for the prevention of air pollution of the Lower Seine at the
level of Rouen) which were in charge of them and which were, at the time,
among the very first to be created in France.
The ALPA and the REMAPPA were united in December 2005 into one and the
same association: Air Normand, since then approved by the State over the
whole of Haute-Normandie.
Air Normand places itself at the service of the general interest and is
committed to respecting the factual character of the data, neutrality in
the interpretation and the diffusion of the results.
2.3. Place
3, place de la Pomme d'Or
80000 Rouen
2.4. Legal status
Association under the law of 1901 approved by the State.
2.5. Functions and activities
The Air Normand association aims to:
- ensure the management and proper functioning of the air quality
monitoring system in Upper Normandy,
- participate in the application of information and alert procedures on
delegation of the Prefect,
- serve as a support for the establishment of actions intended to study,
measure or reduce atmospheric pollution and nuisances and their effects
on health and the environment,
- inform and raise public awareness of air quality problems.
In addition to air quality, the association also evaluates olfactory
nuisances since 1991 by bringing together groups of residents living on
industrial sites: the Nez Normands.
It is governed by the laws on air. Air Normand is an association approved
by the ministry of Ecology and Sustainable Development.
2.7. Internal organisation
24 employees.

Alsace nature


---


1. Identification
1.1 Type of entity
Association
1.2 Authorised form of the name
Alsace Nature
2. Zone of the description
2.1 Dates of existence
1965 - today
2.2 History
The history of Alsace Nature begins on 20 March 1965, around the doctors
Henri-Jean Maresquelle and Henri Ulrich, then president and
vice-president of the association known at the time as the Regional
federative association for the protection of nature (AFRPN). The
necessity of creating such a movement comes from an observation: regional
planning only too rarely takes into consideration its effects on sites,
landscapes, heritage, biological balance and the regulation in matters
of environmental protection is almost non-existent.
The first steps of the AFRPN lead it to the initiation of an inventory of
sites, natural monuments, rare or threatened plants and animals, with the
aim of defining the means to be used to protect them. One of the first
projects would be the setting up of the surveillance of peregrine
falcons.
2.3 Places
Headquarters: 8 rue Adèle Riton in Strasbourg
2.4 Legal status
Association under the law of 1901
2.5 Functions and activities
Alsace Nature is affiliated to France nature environment and is composed
of three entities: the regional federation, Alsace Nature Région and two
departmental federations Alsace Nature 77 and Alsace Nature 68.
Alsace Nature gathers and coordinates the efforts and wills by grouping
the knowledge and skills of nearly 140 federated associations.
As a federation, it mobilises and animates a large network of experts and
volunteers. The reflections produced by the association contribute to the
public debate and bring proposals in more than 300 official bodies.
Alsace Nature alerts and raises awareness of local actors in fields such
as the safeguarding of threatened species, pollution, the preservation of
sites...
3. Zone of relations
3.1. Associated corporate bodies, persons or families
France nature environment
3.2. Type of relations
Member of the network.

environment (AHPNE)
